1	#!/usr/bin/env python
2	"""Isotropic-equivalent energy E_iso for the co-detected FRB sample.
3	
4	For each sightline with a spectroscopic host redshift and a joint CHIME+DSA
5	scattering fit, integrate the fitted fluence spectrum F(nu) = c0 (nu/nu_ref)^gamma
6	over each telescope's OWN observed band, convert each band to an ABSOLUTE Jy
7	scale, k-correct, and sum:
8	
9	    E_iso = (4 pi D_L(z)^2 / (1+z)) [ s_C int_CHIME F_C dnu + s_D int_DSA F_D dnu ]
10	
11	where s_C, s_D are the per-band flux scales (Jy per native .npy fluence unit;
12	`flux_jy_per_unit` in configs/telescopes.yaml).
13	
14	Why the flux scale is mandatory (see analysis/burst_energies/CALIBRATION_REVIEW.md):
15	the joint fit recovers c0 in the input dynamic spectrum's NATIVE units, and CHIME
16	and DSA are not on a common flux standard. c0_C and c0_D are therefore in
17	independent, arbitrary per-telescope units. Summing two such bands is summing
18	dollars and yen: it is not an energy, and the per-burst CHIME:DSA mix varies, so
19	even the relative ORDERING of the total is unreliable. So this script refuses to
20	emit an energy until BOTH bands carry a real `flux_jy_per_unit` (from each
21	telescope's SEFD + beam response at the burst position). Until then it reports the
22	band fluence integrals in native units, flagged uncalibrated, and writes a
23	"pending calibration" LaTeX stub rather than a publishable table of fake erg.
24	
25	The (1+z) bandwidth k-correction (Zhang 2018) is applied by default; the
26	no-k-correction value is tabulated alongside so the manuscript can state the
27	convention explicitly.
28	
29	ref_freq is the band centre (f_min+f_max)/2; the fitter uses median(freq), which
30	equals the centre for a symmetric, uniformly-flagged band (small <~few% offset
31	under asymmetric channel flagging -> minor vs the calibration systematic).
32	
33	Usage:  python analysis/calculate_burst_energies.py [--check]
34	"""
35	
36	from __future__ import annotations
37	
38	import json
39	import sys
40	from pathlib import Path
41	
42	import numpy as np
43	import yaml
44	
45	REPO = Path(__file__).resolve().parents[1]
46	sys.path.insert(0, str(REPO))
47	
48	import astropy.units as u  # noqa: E402
49	
50	from analysis.flux_cal import dsa_band_fluence_jy_ms_hz  # noqa: E402
51	from galaxies.v2_0.config import COSMO, TARGETS  # noqa: E402
52	
53	JOINT_DIR = REPO / "analysis" / "scattering-refit-2026-06" / "joint_json"
54	TEL_CFG = REPO / "configs" / "telescopes.yaml"
55	OUT_DIR = REPO / "analysis" / "burst_energies"
56	
57	JY_MS_HZ_TO_SI = 1e-29  # 1 Jy*ms*Hz = 1e-29 J*m^-2
58	J_TO_ERG = 1e7
59	PLACEHOLDER_Z = 1.0  # Freya/Mahi/Johndoeii carry z=1.0000 as a "redshift unknown" flag
60	
61	
62	def band_edges_hz() -> dict[str, tuple[float, float, float]]:
63	    """(nu1, nu2, nu_ref) in Hz per band from the instrument config (band centre = ref)."""
64	    cfg = yaml.safe_load(TEL_CFG.read_text())
65	    out = {}
66	    for key, tag in (("chime", "C"), ("dsa", "D")):
67	        f1 = cfg[key]["f_min_GHz"] * 1e9
68	        f2 = cfg[key]["f_max_GHz"] * 1e9
69	        out[tag] = (f1, f2, 0.5 * (f1 + f2))
70	    return out
71	
72	
73	def flux_scales() -> dict[str, float | None]:
74	    """tag -> flux_jy_per_unit (Jy per native fluence unit), or None if uncalibrated."""
75	    cfg = yaml.safe_load(TEL_CFG.read_text())
76	    return {tag: cfg[key].get("flux_jy_per_unit") for key, tag in (("chime", "C"), ("dsa", "D"))}
77	
78	
79	def band_integral(c0: float, gamma: float, nu_ref: float, nu1: float, nu2: float) -> float:
80	    """int_{nu1}^{nu2} c0 (nu/nu_ref)^gamma dnu  [native*ms*Hz], freqs in Hz."""
81	    if abs(gamma + 1.0) < 1e-9:  # power law -> 1/nu, integral is a log
82	        return c0 * nu_ref * np.log(nu2 / nu1)
83	    g = gamma + 1.0
84	    return c0 * nu_ref / g * ((nu2 / nu_ref) ** g - (nu1 / nu_ref) ** g)
85	
86	
87	def band_energy_erg(
88	    i_raw: float, flux_scale: float, d_l_m: float, z: float, kcorr: bool = True
89	) -> float:
90	    """Band fluence integral [native*ms*Hz] x flux scale [Jy/native] -> E [erg].
91	
92	    (1+z) bandwidth k-correction (Zhang 2018, ApJ 867 L21) applied when kcorr=True.
93	    """
94	    factor = 1.0 / (1.0 + z) if kcorr else 1.0
95	    return 4.0 * np.pi * d_l_m**2 * i_raw * flux_scale * JY_MS_HZ_TO_SI * J_TO_ERG * factor
96	
97	
98	def load_redshifts() -> dict[str, float]:
99	    """nickname (lowercased) -> z_frb, from the galaxy-search TARGETS list."""
100	    return {name.lower(): z for name, _ra, _dec, z in TARGETS}
101	
102	
103	def load_joint_params() -> dict[str, dict]:
104	    """nickname (lowercased) -> {c0_C, gamma_C, c0_D, gamma_D} medians."""
105	    out = {}
106	    for p in sorted(JOINT_DIR.glob("*_joint_fit.json")):
107	        d = json.loads(p.read_text())
108	        pct = d["percentiles"]
109	        try:
110	            out[d["burst"].lower()] = {
111	                k: pct[k]["median"] for k in ("c0_C", "gamma_C", "c0_D", "gamma_D")
112	            }
113	        except KeyError:
114	            continue  # not a per-band-amplitude fit; skip
115	    return out
116	
117	
118	def _band_jy(scale, i_native, nick, fluence_fn):
119	    """(I_jy_ms_hz or None, is_calibrated) for one band given its scale sentinel.
120	
121	    scale is None -> uncalibrated; the string "fluxcal" -> data-driven per-channel radiometer
122	    integral (fluence_fn(nick), already in Jy*ms*Hz); a float -> legacy native-units power-law
123	    integral times a single Jy-per-native scalar.
124	    """
125	    if scale is None:
126	        return None, False
127	    if scale == "fluxcal":
128	        if fluence_fn is None:
129	            raise NotImplementedError("fluxcal path not wired for this band yet (CHIME is Phase 6)")
130	        return float(fluence_fn(nick)), True
131	    return i_native * float(scale), True
132	
133	
134	def compute(scales: dict[str, float | str | None] | None = None) -> list[dict]:
135	    bands = band_edges_hz()
136	    if scales is None:
137	        scales = flux_scales()
138	    s_C, s_D = scales["C"], scales["D"]
139	    zs = load_redshifts()
140	    fits = load_joint_params()
141	    nu1_C, nu2_C, ref_C = bands["C"]
142	    nu1_D, nu2_D, ref_D = bands["D"]
143	
144	    rows = []
145	    for nick in sorted(fits):
146	        z = zs.get(nick)
147	        if z is None or abs(z - PLACEHOLDER_Z) < 1e-6:
148	            continue  # no real host redshift -> cannot place a distance
149	        fp = fits[nick]
150	        d_l_m = COSMO.luminosity_distance(z).to(u.m).value
151	
152	        I_C = band_integral(fp["c0_C"], fp["gamma_C"], ref_C, nu1_C, nu2_C)
153	        I_D = band_integral(fp["c0_D"], fp["gamma_D"], ref_D, nu1_D, nu2_D)
154	
155	        I_C_jy, cal_C = _band_jy(s_C, I_C, nick, None)  # CHIME fluxcal: Phase 6
156	        I_D_jy, cal_D = _band_jy(s_D, I_D, nick, dsa_band_fluence_jy_ms_hz)
157	        calibrated = cal_C and cal_D
158	
159	        row = {
160	            "burst": nick,
161	            "z": z,
162	            "D_L_Mpc": COSMO.luminosity_distance(z).to(u.Mpc).value,
163	            "c0_C": fp["c0_C"],
164	            "gamma_C": fp["gamma_C"],
165	            "c0_D": fp["c0_D"],
166	            "gamma_D": fp["gamma_D"],
167	            "I_CHIME_native_ms_Hz": I_C,  # band fluence integral, NATIVE units
168	            "I_DSA_native_ms_Hz": I_D,
169	            "calibrated": calibrated,
170	        }
171	        if I_C_jy is not None:
172	            row["I_CHIME_jy_ms_hz"] = I_C_jy
173	        if I_D_jy is not None:
174	            row["I_DSA_jy_ms_hz"] = I_D_jy
175	        if calibrated:
176	            e_C = band_energy_erg(I_C_jy, 1.0, d_l_m, z)  # scale already folded into I_*_jy
177	            e_D = band_energy_erg(I_D_jy, 1.0, d_l_m, z)
178	            e_C0 = band_energy_erg(I_C_jy, 1.0, d_l_m, z, kcorr=False)
179	            e_D0 = band_energy_erg(I_D_jy, 1.0, d_l_m, z, kcorr=False)
180	            row.update(
181	                flux_jy_per_unit_C=s_C,
182	                flux_jy_per_unit_D=s_D,
183	                E_iso_CHIME_erg=e_C,
184	                E_iso_DSA_erg=e_D,
185	                E_iso_erg=e_C + e_D,  # legitimate: both bands now in Jy
186	                E_iso_erg_no_kcorr=e_C0 + e_D0,
187	            )
188	        rows.append(row)
189	    return rows
190	
191	
192	def markdown_table(rows: list[dict]) -> str:
193	    if rows and rows[0]["calibrated"]:
194	        head = (
195	            "| Burst | z | D_L (Mpc) | gamma_C | gamma_D | "
196	            "E_CHIME (erg) | E_DSA (erg) | E_iso (erg) | E_iso no-(1+z) (erg) |"
197	        )
198	        sep = "|" + "|".join(["---"] * 9) + "|"
199	        lines = [head, sep]
200	        for r in rows:
201	            lines.append(
202	                f"| {r['burst']} | {r['z']:.4f} | {r['D_L_Mpc']:.1f} | {r['gamma_C']:.2f} | "
203	                f"{r['gamma_D']:.2f} | {r['E_iso_CHIME_erg']:.3e} | {r['E_iso_DSA_erg']:.3e} | "
204	                f"{r['E_iso_erg']:.3e} | {r['E_iso_erg_no_kcorr']:.3e} |"
205	            )
206	        return "\n".join(lines)
207	    # uncalibrated: band fluence integrals in NATIVE units, flagged not-an-energy. When a band is
208	    # flux-calibrated but the other is not (gate closed), also surface its Jy*ms*Hz integral.
209	    has_dsa_jy = any("I_DSA_jy_ms_hz" in r for r in rows)
210	    head = (
211	        "| Burst | z | D_L (Mpc) | gamma_C | gamma_D | "
212	        "int F_C (native*ms*Hz) | int F_D (native*ms*Hz) |"
213	    )
214	    if has_dsa_jy:
215	        head += " int F_D (Jy*ms*Hz) |"
216	    sep = "|" + "|".join(["---"] * (8 if has_dsa_jy else 7)) + "|"
217	    lines = [head, sep]
218	    for r in rows:
219	        line = (
220	            f"| {r['burst']} | {r['z']:.4f} | {r['D_L_Mpc']:.1f} | {r['gamma_C']:.2f} | "
221	            f"{r['gamma_D']:.2f} | {r['I_CHIME_native_ms_Hz']:.3e} | {r['I_DSA_native_ms_Hz']:.3e} |"
222	        )
223	        if has_dsa_jy:
224	            jy = r.get("I_DSA_jy_ms_hz")
225	            line += f" {jy:.3e} |" if jy is not None else " -- |"
226	        lines.append(line)
227	    return "\n".join(lines)
228	
229	
230	def _tex_pow(x: float) -> str:
231	    r"""Render a float as a\times10^{b} for LaTeX."""
232	    if x <= 0 or not np.isfinite(x):
233	        return r"\mathrm{n/a}"
234	    exp = int(np.floor(np.log10(x)))
235	    mant = x / 10**exp
236	    return rf"{mant:.2f}\times10^{{{exp}}}"
237	
238	
239	def latex_pending() -> str:
240	    """LaTeX stub written while the flux calibration is missing (no fake energies)."""
241	    return r"""% Auto-generated by analysis/calculate_burst_energies.py -- do not hand-edit.
242	% STATUS: flux calibration pending -- no energy table emitted (see below).
243	\subsection{Isotropic-equivalent energies}
244	\label{sec:burst-energies}
245	
246	Isotropic-equivalent burst energies are pending absolute flux calibration. The
247	joint CHIME--DSA fit constrains each band's fluence spectrum only in the native
248	units of its input dynamic spectrum, and CHIME and DSA are not placed on a common
249	flux standard, so the per-band amplitudes are not directly comparable and may not
250	be summed into an energy. Converting to physical $\mathrm{Jy\,ms}$ requires each
251	telescope's system-equivalent flux density and primary-beam response at the burst
252	position (Andersen et~al.\ 2023 and the baseband CHIME/FRB catalog update for
253	CHIME; Law et~al.\ 2024 for DSA-110). Once those scales are supplied, the energy
254	follows $E_{\mathrm{iso}} = (4\pi D_L^2(z)/(1+z))\,[\,s_C\!\int F_C\,d\nu +
255	s_D\!\int F_D\,d\nu\,]$ with the $(1+z)$ bandwidth k-correction.
256	"""
257	
258	
259	def latex_section(rows: list[dict]) -> str:
260	    body = []
261	    for r in rows:
262	        body.append(
263	            f"  {r['burst'].capitalize()} & ${r['z']:.4f}$ & ${r['D_L_Mpc']:.0f}$ & "
264	            f"${r['gamma_C']:+.2f}$ & ${r['gamma_D']:+.2f}$ & "
265	            f"${_tex_pow(r['E_iso_erg'])}$ \\\\"
266	        )
267	    table_body = "\n".join(body)
268	    return rf"""% Auto-generated by analysis/calculate_burst_energies.py -- do not hand-edit.
269	\subsection{{Isotropic-equivalent energies}}
270	\label{{sec:burst-energies}}
271	
272	For each sightline with a spectroscopic host redshift we estimate the
273	isotropic-equivalent burst energy directly from the joint CHIME--DSA fit,
274	without extrapolating either band's spectrum beyond where it is constrained.
275	The joint fit returns a per-band spectral amplitude and index,
276	$F_X(\nu) = c_{{0,X}}\,(\nu/\nu_{{\mathrm{{ref}},X}})^{{\gamma_X}}$, which we put on
277	an absolute scale with each telescope's flux calibration $s_X$
278	($\mathrm{{Jy}}$ per native unit) and integrate over its own observing
279	band---CHIME over $0.400$--$0.800\,\mathrm{{GHz}}$ and DSA over
280	$1.311$--$1.499\,\mathrm{{GHz}}$,
281	%
282	\begin{{equation}}
283	E_{{\mathrm{{iso}}}} = \frac{{4\pi D_L^2(z)}}{{1+z}}\left[
284	  s_C\!\int_{{\nu_1^{{C}}}}^{{\nu_2^{{C}}}} F_{{\mathrm{{CHIME}}}}(\nu)\,d\nu
285	  + s_D\!\int_{{\nu_1^{{D}}}}^{{\nu_2^{{D}}}} F_{{\mathrm{{DSA}}}}(\nu)\,d\nu
286	\right],
287	\label{{eq:eiso}}
288	\end{{equation}}
289	%
290	with $D_L(z)$ from the fiducial \textit{{Planck}}\,2018 cosmology, the $(1+z)$
291	bandwidth k-correction applied, and
292	$1\,\mathrm{{Jy\,ms\,Hz}} = 10^{{-29}}\,\mathrm{{J\,m^{{-2}}}}$. Bursts whose hosts
293	lack a spectroscopic redshift are omitted. This band-restricted integral
294	constrains the energy released \emph{{within the observed spectral envelope}}: it
295	avoids the large, model-dependent extrapolation that a single power law fit
296	across the full $0.4$--$1.5\,\mathrm{{GHz}}$ span would require, at the cost of not
297	counting flux in the unobserved $0.8$--$1.3\,\mathrm{{GHz}}$ gap (so the values are
298	lower limits on the $0.4$--$1.5\,\mathrm{{GHz}}$ energy).
299	
300	\begin{{table}}
301	\centering
302	\caption{{Isotropic-equivalent energies of the co-detected FRBs, integrated over
303	the CHIME ($0.4$--$0.8\,\mathrm{{GHz}}$) and DSA ($1.31$--$1.50\,\mathrm{{GHz}}$) bands
304	from the joint scattering fit and put on an absolute scale with each band's flux
305	calibration. $\gamma_C,\gamma_D$ are the per-band spectral indices;
306	$E_{{\mathrm{{iso}}}}$ includes the $(1+z)$ k-correction.}}
307	\label{{tab:burst-energies}}
308	\begin{{tabular}}{{lccccc}}
309	\hline\hline
310	Burst & $z$ & $D_L$ [Mpc] & $\gamma_{{C}}$ & $\gamma_{{D}}$ & $E_{{\mathrm{{iso}}}}$ [erg] \\
311	\hline
312	{table_body}
313	\hline
314	\end{{tabular}}
315	\end{{table}}
316	"""
317	
318	
319	def _check() -> None:
320	    # 1. closed-form band integral must match a dense numeric quadrature
321	    nu1, nu2, ref = 0.4e9, 0.8e9, 0.6e9
322	    for c0, g in ((2.0, -3.4), (0.18, -4.95), (1.0, -1.0), (3.0, 0.5)):
323	        nu = np.linspace(nu1, nu2, 200_001)
324	        num = np.trapezoid(c0 * (nu / ref) ** g, nu)
325	        ana = band_integral(c0, g, ref, nu1, nu2)
326	        assert abs(ana - num) / abs(num) < 1e-6, (c0, g, ana, num)
327	
328	    # 2. oracle: flat spectrum (gamma=0, c0=1) over a known band, scale=1, gives the
329	    #    analytic energy; (1+z) divides it; doubling the flux scale doubles it.
330	    i_flat = band_integral(1.0, 0.0, ref, nu1, nu2)
331	    assert abs(i_flat - (nu2 - nu1)) < 1e-3, i_flat  # int 1 dnu = bandwidth
332	    e0 = band_energy_erg(i_flat, 1.0, d_l_m=1.0, z=0.0)
333	    assert abs(e0 - 4.0 * np.pi * (nu2 - nu1) * JY_MS_HZ_TO_SI * J_TO_ERG) < 1e-6 * e0
334	    assert abs(band_energy_erg(i_flat, 1.0, 1.0, 1.0) - e0 / 2.0) < 1e-9 * e0  # (1+z)=2
335	    assert abs(band_energy_erg(i_flat, 2.0, 1.0, 0.0) - 2.0 * e0) < 1e-9 * e0  # linear in scale
336	
337	    # 3. the gate itself, both directions (inject scales; independent of the live config)
338	    assert not any("E_iso_erg" in r for r in compute(scales={"C": None, "D": 1.0})), (
339	        "partial calibration leaked an energy"
340	    )
341	    cal_rows = compute(scales={"C": 1.0, "D": 1.0})
342	    assert cal_rows and all("E_iso_erg" in r for r in cal_rows), "calibrated run emitted no energy"
343	    r = cal_rows[0]
344	    assert abs(r["E_iso_erg"] - r["E_iso_erg_no_kcorr"] / (1.0 + r["z"])) < 1e-9 * r["E_iso_erg"]
345	    # and the live config must not be half-set (would silently drop one band's flux)
346	    live = flux_scales()
347	    assert (live["C"] is None) == (live["D"] is None), (
348	        "telescopes.yaml: calibrate both bands or neither"
349	    )
350	    print("self-check OK: integral matches quadrature; energy oracle, k-correction, and gate exact")
351	
352	
353	def main() -> None:
354	    if "--check" in sys.argv:
355	        _check()
356	        return
357	
358	    rows = compute()
359	    if not rows:
360	        sys.exit("no bursts with both a real redshift and a joint c0/gamma fit")
361	
362	    OUT_DIR.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
363	    (OUT_DIR / "burst_energies.json").write_text(json.dumps(rows, indent=2))
364	    calibrated = rows[0]["calibrated"]
365	    tex = latex_section(rows) if calibrated else latex_pending()
366	    (OUT_DIR / "burst_energies.tex").write_text(tex)
367	
368	    print(markdown_table(rows))
369	    print(
370	        f"\nN = {len(rows)} bursts.  "
371	        f"Skipped: placeholder z=1.0 (freya/mahi/johndoeii) and any sightline "
372	        f"without a joint c0/gamma fit (e.g. casey)."
373	    )
374	    if not calibrated:
375	        print(
376	            "\n*** UNCALIBRATED: configs/telescopes.yaml has no flux_jy_per_unit for "
377	            "one or both bands. ***\n"
378	            "The columns above are band fluence integrals in NATIVE .npy units -- NOT "
379	            "energies, NOT comparable across bands, NOT to be quoted. Supply "
380	            "flux_jy_per_unit (Jy per native unit, from each telescope's SEFD + beam "
381	            "response at the burst position) for BOTH bands to emit E_iso. See "
382	            "analysis/burst_energies/CALIBRATION_REVIEW.md."
383	        )
384	    print(f"\nWrote {OUT_DIR / 'burst_energies.json'}")
385	    print(
386	        f"Wrote {OUT_DIR / 'burst_energies.tex'} ({'energy table' if calibrated else 'calibration-pending stub'})"
387	    )
388	
389	
390	if __name__ == "__main__":
391	    main()
392

378	def _joint_prior_spec(
379	    init_C: FRBParams,
380	    init_D: FRBParams,
381	    alpha_bounds: tuple[float, float],
382	) -> list[tuple[str, tuple[float, float], bool]]:
383	    """Assemble per-index (name, (lo, hi), is_log) from the single-band priors.
384	
385	    Reuses build_priors(absolute_bounds=True) per telescope so the joint prior is
386	    init-independent (required for a global sampler) except t0, whose window is
387	    anchored on each band's data profile-peak estimate. alpha is widened to
388	    alpha_bounds to allow shallower-than-Kolmogorov slopes (the whole point).
389	    """
390	    pC, _ = build_priors(init_C, absolute_bounds=True)
391	    pD, _ = build_priors(init_D, absolute_bounds=True)
392	    # tau_1ghz bound is the absolute WIDTH_MIN..WIDTH_MAX (identical in pC/pD).
393	    by_name = {
394	        "tau_1ghz": pC["tau_1ghz"],
395	        "alpha": tuple(alpha_bounds),
396	        "c0_C": pC["c0"],
397	        "t0_C": pC["t0"],
398	        "gamma_C": pC["gamma"],
399	        "zeta_C": pC["zeta"],
400	        "delta_dm_C": pC["delta_dm"],
401	        "c0_D": pD["c0"],
402	        "t0_D": pD["t0"],
403	        "gamma_D": pD["gamma"],
404	        "zeta_D": pD["zeta"],
405	        "delta_dm_D": pD["delta_dm"],
406	    }
407	    return [(n, by_name[n], n in _LOG_NAMES) for n in JOINT_PARAM_NAMES]
408	
409	
410	def _joint_prior_spec_gain(
411	    init_C: FRBParams,
412	    init_D: FRBParams,
413	    alpha_bounds: tuple[float, float],
414	) -> list[tuple[str, tuple[float, float], bool]]:
415	    """Prior spec for the 8-vector gain-marginalized fit (no c0, gamma)."""
416	    pC, _ = build_priors(init_C, absolute_bounds=True)
417	    pD, _ = build_priors(init_D, absolute_bounds=True)
418	    by_name = {
419	        "tau_1ghz": pC["tau_1ghz"],
420	        "alpha": tuple(alpha_bounds),
421	        "t0_C": pC["t0"],
422	        "zeta_C": pC["zeta"],
423	        "delta_dm_C": pC["delta_dm"],
424	        "t0_D": pD["t0"],
425	        "zeta_D": pD["zeta"],
426	        "delta_dm_D": pD["delta_dm"],
427	    }
428	    return [(n, by_name[n], n in _LOG_NAMES_GAIN) for n in JOINT_PARAM_NAMES_GAIN]
429	
430	
431	def _joint_prior_spec_gain_gp(
432	    init_C: FRBParams,
433	    init_D: FRBParams,
434	    alpha_bounds: tuple[float, float],
435	    model_C: FRBModel,
436	    model_D: FRBModel,
437	) -> list[tuple[str, tuple[float, float], bool]]:
438	    """Prior spec for the 10-vector gain+scintillation-GP fit.
439	
440	    Reuses the 8 temporal entries from `_joint_prior_spec_gain`, then appends a
441	    per-band log-uniform Delta_nu_d with bounds [0.3*chan_width, band/3] computed
442	    from each model's freq axis (data-derived, not hardcoded).
443	    """
444	    base = {n: (b, lg) for n, b, lg in _joint_prior_spec_gain(init_C, init_D, alpha_bounds)}
445	    dnu_C = _dnu_d_bounds(model_C.freq)
446	    dnu_D = _dnu_d_bounds(model_D.freq)
447	    by_name = dict(base)
448	    by_name["Delta_nu_d_C"] = (dnu_C, True)
449	    by_name["Delta_nu_d_D"] = (dnu_D, True)
450	    return [(n, by_name[n][0], n in _LOG_NAMES_GAIN_GP) for n in JOINT_PARAM_NAMES_GAIN_GP]
451	
452	
453	def _joint_prior_spec_gain_shared_zeta(
454	    init_C: FRBParams,
455	    init_D: FRBParams,
456	    alpha_bounds: tuple[float, float],
457	    x_bounds: tuple[float, float] = (-4.0, 2.0),
458	) -> list[tuple[str, tuple[float, float], bool]]:
459	    """Prior spec for the 8-vector shared-zeta(nu) gain-marginal fit.
460	
461	    zeta_1ghz reuses the UNION of both bands' absolute zeta bounds -- the width at
462	    1 GHz sits in the inter-band gap, so neither band alone bounds it. x_zeta is a
463	    SIGNED power-law index (uniform, not log); default (-4, 2) is deliberately wide
464	    enough that intrinsic width CAN mimic scattering (the degeneracy the validation
465	    probes) yet caps high-freq broadening at a physical limit.
466	    """
467	    pC, _ = build_priors(init_C, absolute_bounds=True)
468	    pD, _ = build_priors(init_D, absolute_bounds=True)
469	    z_union = (min(pC["zeta"][0], pD["zeta"][0]), max(pC["zeta"][1], pD["zeta"][1]))
470	    by_name = {
471	        "tau_1ghz": pC["tau_1ghz"],
472	        "alpha": tuple(alpha_bounds),
473	        "zeta_1ghz": z_union,
474	        "x_zeta": tuple(x_bounds),
475	        "t0_C": pC["t0"],
476	        "delta_dm_C": pC["delta_dm"],
477	        "t0_D": pD["t0"],
478	        "delta_dm_D": pD["delta_dm"],
479	    }
480	    return [(n, by_name[n], n in _LOG_NAMES_GAIN_SZ) for n in JOINT_PARAM_NAMES_GAIN_SHARED_ZETA]
481	
482	
483	def _joint_prior_spec_gain_multi(
484	    init_C: FRBParams,
485	    init_D: FRBParams,
486	    alpha_bounds: tuple[float, float],
487	    n_C: int = 1,
488	    n_D: int = 1,
489	) -> list[tuple[str, tuple[float, float], bool]]:
490	    """Prior spec for the N-component-per-band gain-marginal fit.
491	
492	    Each component reuses the single-band (t0, zeta) bounds; the t0 window is the
493	    SAME for every component in a band (the ordered transform separates them).
494	    """
495	    pC, _ = build_priors(init_C, absolute_bounds=True)
496	    pD, _ = build_priors(init_D, absolute_bounds=True)
497	    spec: list[tuple[str, tuple[float, float], bool]] = [
498	        ("tau_1ghz", pC["tau_1ghz"], True),
499	        ("alpha", tuple(alpha_bounds), False),
500	    ]
501	    for i in range(1, int(n_C) + 1):
502	        spec.append((f"t0_C{i}", pC["t0"], False))
503	        spec.append((f"zeta_C{i}", pC["zeta"], True))
504	    spec.append(("delta_dm_C", pC["delta_dm"], False))
505	    for i in range(1, int(n_D) + 1):
506	        spec.append((f"t0_D{i}", pD["t0"], False))
507	        spec.append((f"zeta_D{i}", pD["zeta"], True))
508	    spec.append(("delta_dm_D", pD["delta_dm"], False))
509	    return spec
510	
511	
512	class _JointPriorTransform:
513	    """Picklable unit-cube -> parameter transform for the 12-vector.
514	
515	    Module-level callable (not a closure) so dynesty.pool can ship it to workers.
516	    Log-uniform on the flagged positive params, uniform on the rest.
517	    """
518	
519	    def __init__(self, spec: list[tuple[str, tuple[float, float], bool]]):
520	        self.lo = np.array([s[1][0] for s in spec], dtype=float)
521	        self.hi = np.array([s[1][1] for s in spec], dtype=float)
522	        # only log-sample where flagged AND both bounds strictly positive
523	        self.is_log = np.array([bool(s[2] and s[1][0] > 0 and s[1][1] > 0) for s in spec])
524	        # precompute log-bounds with safe placeholders (log(1)=0) on linear axes
525	        self._loglo = np.log(np.where(self.is_log, self.lo, 1.0))
526	        self._loghi = np.log(np.where(self.is_log, self.hi, 1.0))
527	
528	    def __call__(self, u: NDArray[np.floating]) -> NDArray[np.floating]:
529	        lin = self.lo + u * (self.hi - self.lo)
530	        logu = np.exp(self._loglo + u * (self._loghi - self._loglo))
531	        return np.where(self.is_log, logu, lin)
532	
533	
534	class _JointPriorTransformOrdered(_JointPriorTransform):

827	    *,
828	    model_C: FRBModel,
829	    init_C: FRBParams,
830	    model_D: FRBModel,
831	    init_D: FRBParams,
832	    alpha_bounds: tuple[float, float] = (2.0, 6.0),
833	    nlive: int = 600,
834	    dlogz: float = 0.5,
835	    nproc: int | None = None,
836	    sample: str = "rwalk",
837	    verbose: bool = True,
838	    marginalize_gain: bool = False,
839	    marginalize_gain_gp: bool = False,
840	    # NOTE: the run_joint_fit CLI now defaults this to True (--per-band-zeta is the opt-out).
841	    # Per-band zeta (False) is the legacy path and lets the intrinsic width absorb cross-band
842	    # structure, corrupting the shared-alpha lever arm (JOINT_FIT_STATE.md). New direct callers
843	    # should pass shared_zeta=True; the False default here is kept only for API stability.
844	    shared_zeta: bool = False,
845	    x_zeta_bounds: tuple[float, float] = (-4.0, 2.0),
846	    mu_degree: int = 1,
847	    components_C: int = 1,
848	    components_D: int = 1,
849	    gain_s2: float | None = None,
850	    dt_min: float | None = None,
851	    force_multi: bool = False,
852	    **dynesty_kwargs,
853	) -> dict[str, Any]:
854	    """Run the joint CHIME+DSA nested fit; return posterior summary.
855	
856	    Parameters
857	    ----------
858	    model_C, model_D : FRBModel
859	        CHIME and DSA burst models, each with data + noise loaded.
860	    init_C, init_D : FRBParams
861	        Per-band data-driven inits (used only to anchor the t0 prior window and
862	        scale-free absolute bounds).
863	    alpha_bounds : (lo, hi)
864	        Uniform prior on the shared scattering index. Default (2, 6) is wide
865	        enough to detect shallow (sub-Kolmogorov) slopes.
866	    nlive, dlogz, nproc, sample
867	        dynesty knobs (12-dim -> nlive ~600+ recommended).
868	
869	    Returns
870	    -------
871	    dict with keys: param_names, percentiles, log_evidence, log_evidence_err,
872	    samples, weights, alpha_bounds.
873	    """
874	    from dynesty import NestedSampler
875	
876	    if model_C.data is None or model_D.data is None:
877	        raise ValueError("both FRBModels must have data loaded")
878	
879	    multi = bool(force_multi) or int(components_C) > 1 or int(components_D) > 1
880	    ptform = None
881	    if multi:
882	        names = JOINT_PARAM_NAMES_GAIN_MULTI(components_C, components_D)
883	        spec = _joint_prior_spec_gain_multi(
884	            init_C, init_D, alpha_bounds, components_C, components_D
885	        )
886	        loglike = _JointLogLikelihoodGainMulti(
887	            model_C, model_D, n_C=components_C, n_D=components_D, s2=gain_s2
888	        )
889	        # dt_min: a few channel time-samples of each band (data-derived), one floor
890	        # PER band ([dt_C, dt_D]) so each band's components are bound by its own time
891	        # resolution. An explicit scalar dt_min is broadcast to both groups.
892	        if dt_min is None:
893	            dt_min = [
894	                float(np.median(np.abs(np.diff(np.asarray(m.time, dtype=float))))) * 3.0
895	                for m in (model_C, model_D)
896	            ]
897	        # index groups of each band's t0 components within the vector.
898	        idx = {n: i for i, n in enumerate(names)}
899	        grp_C = [idx[f"t0_C{i}"] for i in range(1, int(components_C) + 1)]
900	        grp_D = [idx[f"t0_D{i}"] for i in range(1, int(components_D) + 1)]
901	        ptform = _JointPriorTransformOrdered(spec, [grp_C, grp_D], dt_min=dt_min)
902	    elif shared_zeta:
903	        names = JOINT_PARAM_NAMES_GAIN_SHARED_ZETA
904	        spec = _joint_prior_spec_gain_shared_zeta(init_C, init_D, alpha_bounds, x_zeta_bounds)
905	        loglike = _JointLogLikelihoodGainSharedZeta(model_C, model_D)
906	    elif marginalize_gain_gp:
907	        names = JOINT_PARAM_NAMES_GAIN_GP
908	        spec = _joint_prior_spec_gain_gp(init_C, init_D, alpha_bounds, model_C, model_D)
909	        loglike = _JointLogLikelihoodGainGP(model_C, model_D, mu_degree=mu_degree)
910	    elif marginalize_gain:
911	        names = JOINT_PARAM_NAMES_GAIN
912	        spec = _joint_prior_spec_gain(init_C, init_D, alpha_bounds)
913	        loglike = _JointLogLikelihoodGain(model_C, model_D)
914	    else:
915	        names = JOINT_PARAM_NAMES
916	        spec = _joint_prior_spec(init_C, init_D, alpha_bounds)
917	        loglike = _JointLogLikelihood(model_C, model_D)
918	    ndim = len(spec)
919	    if ptform is None:
920	        ptform = _JointPriorTransform(spec)
921	    if ndim >= 12 and nlive < 800:
922	        log.info(
923	            f"Joint multi-component fit ndim={ndim} >= 12: recommend "
924	            f"nlive>=800-1000 (got {nlive}) for reliable evidence."
925	        )
926	
927	    if verbose:
928	        log.info(
929	            f"Joint CHIME+DSA fit: ndim={ndim}, nlive={nlive}, "
930	            f"alpha~U{alpha_bounds}, marginalize_gain={marginalize_gain}, "
931	            f"marginalize_gain_gp={marginalize_gain_gp}, shared_zeta={shared_zeta}"
932	        )
933	
934	    if nproc is not None and nproc > 1:
935	        # fork so workers inherit memory instead of re-importing __main__ (spawn
936	        # default crashes); identical pattern to burstfit_nested.
937	        import multiprocessing as _mp
938	
939	        try:
940	            _mp.set_start_method("fork", force=True)
941	        except RuntimeError:
942	            pass
943	        from dynesty import pool as dypool
944	
945	        with dypool.Pool(int(nproc), loglike, ptform) as pool:
946	            sampler = NestedSampler(
947	                pool.loglike,
948	                pool.prior_transform,
949	                ndim,
950	                nlive=nlive,
951	                sample=sample,
952	                pool=pool,
953	                queue_size=int(nproc),
954	                **dynesty_kwargs,
955	            )
956	            sampler.run_nested(dlogz=dlogz, print_progress=verbose)
957	            results = sampler.results
958	    else:
959	        sampler = NestedSampler(loglike, ptform, ndim, nlive=nlive, sample=sample, **dynesty_kwargs)
960	        sampler.run_nested(dlogz=dlogz, print_progress=verbose)
961	        results = sampler.results
962	
963	    weights = np.exp(results.logwt - results.logz[-1])
964	    weights /= weights.sum()
965	
966	    return {
967	        "param_names": list(names),
968	        "percentiles": _weighted_percentiles(results.samples, weights, names),
969	        "log_evidence": float(results.logz[-1]),
970	        "log_evidence_err": float(results.logzerr[-1]),
971	        "samples": results.samples,
972	        "weights": weights,
973	        "alpha_bounds": tuple(alpha_bounds),
974	        "ncall": int(np.sum(results.ncall)),  # dynesty .ncall is per-iteration, sum for total
975	    }
976	
977	
978	def demo() -> None:
979	    """Self-check: the shared-tau likelihood must prefer the true alpha.
980	
981	    Builds two synthetic single-band bursts (CHIME 0.6 / DSA 1.4 GHz) scattered
982	    with a common tau_1ghz, alpha_true, then verifies the joint log-likelihood
983	    (profiled crudely over the per-band amplitudes only) peaks at alpha_true and
984	    rejects a wrong alpha. No sampler -- a fast logic gate, not a fit.
985	    """
```

42	def _cluster_mask(df: pd.DataFrame) -> pd.Series:
43	    """Per-row True where a classification column marks the object a galaxy cluster."""
44	    is_cluster = pd.Series(False, index=df.index)
45	    for column in CLASSIFICATION_COLUMNS:
46	        if column not in df.columns:
47	            continue
48	        is_cluster = is_cluster | df[column].astype(str).str.contains(_CLUSTER_RE)
49	    return is_cluster
50	
51	
52	def _first_available_numeric(df: pd.DataFrame, columns: tuple[str, ...]) -> pd.Series:
53	    values = pd.Series(math.nan, index=df.index, dtype="float64")
54	    for column in columns:
55	        if column not in df.columns:
56	            continue
57	        candidate = pd.to_numeric(df[column], errors="coerce")
58	        values = values.where(values.notna(), candidate)
59	    return values
60	
61	
62	def _foreground_mask(
63	    df: pd.DataFrame,
64	    z_frb: float,
65	    z_eps: float,
66	    impact_kpc: float,
67	    cluster_impact_kpc: float = DEFAULT_CLUSTER_IMPACT_KPC,
68	    n_sigma: float = 2.0,
69	    photoz_floor: float = FOREGROUND_PHOTOZ_FLOOR,
70	    max_photoz_err: float = MAX_PHOTOZ_Z_ERR,
71	) -> pd.Series:
72	    z = pd.to_numeric(df["z"], errors="coerce")
73	    impact = pd.to_numeric(df["impact_kpc"], errors="coerce")
74	    z_err = _first_available_numeric(df, PHOTO_Z_ERROR_COLUMNS)
75	    has_z_err = z_err.notna() & (z_err > 0.0)
76	    z_limit = z_frb + z_eps
77	
78	    # Spec-z rows (no photo-z error: NED, GLADE+) use a plain z < z_limit with no
79	    # floor, so genuine nearby spec galaxies (e.g. UGC 06371, z=0.009) survive.
80	    spec_foreground = ~has_z_err & (z < z_limit)
81	    # Photo-z rows (DESI VII/292): require a credible floor (drops the z~0 photo-z
82	    # pile-up) and a 2-sigma lower bound below z_limit computed with the error
83	    # CAPPED — DESI floor/leak rows carry sigma_z = 0.4-1.7 that would otherwise
84	    # rescue any background galaxy as "foreground".
85	    z_err_capped = z_err.clip(upper=max_photoz_err)
86	    photo_foreground = has_z_err & (z >= photoz_floor) & ((z - n_sigma * z_err_capped) < z_limit)
87	    foreground = spec_foreground | photo_foreground
88	    # Clusters get a mass-scaled r200 impact limit; galaxies keep impact_kpc.
89	    impact_limit = _cluster_impact_limit_kpc(df, impact_kpc, fallback_kpc=cluster_impact_kpc)
90	    return foreground & (impact <= impact_limit)
91	
92	
93	def _cluster_impact_limit_kpc(
94	    df: pd.DataFrame,
95	    impact_kpc: float,
96	    r200_factor: float = CLUSTER_R200_FACTOR,
97	    fallback_kpc: float = DEFAULT_CLUSTER_IMPACT_KPC,
98	) -> pd.Series:
99	    """Per-row impact limit (kpc).
100	
101	    Galaxies keep ``impact_kpc``. A cluster row with a catalog M500 gets
102	    ``r200_factor * r200`` (r200 from M200 = CLUSTER_M500_TO_M200 * M500); a cluster
103	    row without a catalog mass (e.g. NED-Type only) falls back to ``fallback_kpc``.
104	    """
105	    limit = pd.Series(float(impact_kpc), index=df.index, dtype="float64")
106	    is_cluster = _cluster_mask(df)
107	    m500 = (
108	        pd.to_numeric(df["m500_msun"], errors="coerce")
109	        if "m500_msun" in df.columns
110	        else pd.Series(math.nan, index=df.index)
111	    )
112	    z = pd.to_numeric(df["z"], errors="coerce")
113	    for i in df.index[is_cluster]:
114	        mi = m500.get(i, math.nan)
115	        zi = z.get(i, math.nan)
116	        if pd.notna(mi) and mi > 0.0 and pd.notna(zi) and zi > 0.0:
117	            r200 = scat.r_delta_kpc(CLUSTER_M500_TO_M200 * float(mi), float(zi), 200)
118	            limit.at[i] = r200_factor * r200 if math.isfinite(r200) else fallback_kpc
119	        else:
120	            limit.at[i] = fallback_kpc
121	    return limit
122	
123	
124	def _redshift_error(row: pd.Series) -> float:
125	    for column in PHOTO_Z_ERROR_COLUMNS:
126	        if column not in row.index:
127	            continue
128	        value = pd.to_numeric(pd.Series([row[column]]), errors="coerce").iloc[0]
129	        if pd.notna(value) and value > 0.0:
130	            return float(value)
131	    return math.inf
132	
133	
134	def _catalog_rank(catalog: object) -> int:
135	    catalog_text = str(catalog).lower()
136	    if "ned" in catalog_text or "sdss" in catalog_text:
137	        return 0
138	    if "glade" in catalog_text or "vii/291" in catalog_text:
139	        return 1
140	    if "desi" in catalog_text or "vii/292" in catalog_text:
141	        return 2
142	    return 3
143	
144	
145	def _is_duplicate(row_a: pd.Series, row_b: pd.Series, coord_a: SkyCoord, coord_b: SkyCoord) -> bool:
146	    sep = coord_a.separation(coord_b)
147	    if sep >= DUPLICATE_SEPARATION:
148	        return False
149	
150	    z_a = float(row_a["z"])
151	    z_b = float(row_b["z"])
152	    scaled_delta_z = abs(z_a - z_b) / (1.0 + min(z_a, z_b))
153	    return scaled_delta_z < DUPLICATE_REDSHIFT_TOLERANCE
154

157	    rows = [matches.iloc[pos] for pos in positions]
158	    finite_error_positions = [
159	        pos for pos, row in zip(positions, rows) if math.isfinite(_redshift_error(row))

167	def read_measured_tau_ms(fit_json_path: str | None) -> float | None:
168	    """Return tau_1ghz (ms) from a scat_analysis fit_results.json, else None.
169	
170	    This is a raw parser: it returns the best-fit tau regardless of fit quality.
171	    Use read_tau_fit for the quality-gated value with uncertainties.
172	    """
173	    if not fit_json_path or not os.path.exists(fit_json_path):
174	        return None
175	    try:
176	        with open(fit_json_path) as fh:
177	            data = json.load(fh)
178	    except (OSError, ValueError):
179	        return None
180	    params = data.get("best_params", {}) if isinstance(data, dict) else {}
181	    tau = params.get("tau_1ghz")
182	    tau_f = _f(tau)
183	    if not math.isfinite(tau_f) or tau_f <= 0.0:
184	        return None
185	    return float(tau_f)
186	
187	
188	def read_tau_fit(fit_json_path: str | None) -> dict | None:
189	    """Read tau_1ghz with uncertainty and quality flag from a fit_results.json.
190	
191	    Prefers the posterior median + 16/84 from best_params_percentiles (the
192	    uncertainty-bearing summary); falls back to the best_params point estimate.
193	    Returns {tau, err_minus, err_plus, quality_flag, chi2_reduced} or None when no
194	    usable tau is present. The quality_flag is the pipeline's recalibrated
195	    chi2-driven gate (PASS/MARGINAL/FAIL); callers decide whether to ingest.
196	    """
197	    if not fit_json_path or not os.path.exists(fit_json_path):
198	        return None
199	    try:
200	        with open(fit_json_path) as fh:
201	            data = json.load(fh)
202	    except (OSError, ValueError):
203	        return None
204	    if not isinstance(data, dict):
205	        return None
206	
207	    err_minus = err_plus = math.nan
208	    tau_f = math.nan
209	    pct = data.get("best_params_percentiles")
210	    if isinstance(pct, dict) and isinstance(pct.get("tau_1ghz"), dict):
211	        tau_pct = pct["tau_1ghz"]
212	        tau_f = _f(tau_pct.get("median"))
213	        err_minus = _f(tau_pct.get("err_minus"))
214	        err_plus = _f(tau_pct.get("err_plus"))
215	    if not math.isfinite(tau_f):
216	        params = data.get("best_params", {}) or {}
217	        tau_f = _f(params.get("tau_1ghz"))
218	    if not math.isfinite(tau_f) or tau_f <= 0.0:
219	        return None
220	
221	    gof = data.get("goodness_of_fit", {}) or {}
222	    return {
223	        "tau": float(tau_f),
224	        "err_minus": err_minus,
225	        "err_plus": err_plus,
226	        "quality_flag": gof.get("quality_flag"),
227	        "chi2_reduced": _f(gof.get("chi2_reduced")),
228	    }
229	
230	
231	_PYGEDM: Any = None
232	
233	
234	def _load_pygedm() -> Any:
235	    """Import pygedm once, shimming the SciPy>=1.14 removal of integrate.simps."""
236	    global _PYGEDM
237	    if _PYGEDM is not None:
238	        return _PYGEDM
239	    try:
240	        import scipy.integrate as _si
241	
242	        if not hasattr(_si, "simps"):
243	            # pygedm.yt2020 still calls the removed scipy.integrate.simps; the
244	            # successor simpson made x keyword-only, so wrap rather than alias.
245	            _si.simps = lambda y, x=None, *a, **k: _si.simpson(y, x=x, *a, **k)
246	        import warnings
247	
248	        with warnings.catch_warnings():
249	            warnings.simplefilter("ignore")
250	            import pygedm
251	        _PYGEDM = pygedm
252	    except Exception:
253	        _PYGEDM = False
254	    return _PYGEDM
255	
256	
257	def galactic_dm_tau(l_deg: float, b_deg: float, method: str = "ne2001", dist_pc: float = 30000.0):
258	    """Return (DM_NE2001, DM_YMW16, tau_MW_ms) toward Galactic (l, b).
259	
260	    Uses the compiled pygedm NE2001/YMW16 models (offline). Returns NaNs if
261	    pygedm is unavailable so callers degrade gracefully.
262	    """
263	    pg = _load_pygedm()
264	    if not pg:
265	        return (math.nan, math.nan, math.nan)
266	    import warnings
267	
268	    with warnings.catch_warnings():
269	        warnings.simplefilter("ignore")
270	        dm_ne, tau_ne = pg.dist_to_dm(l_deg, b_deg, dist_pc, method="ne2001")
271	        dm_yw, tau_yw = pg.dist_to_dm(l_deg, b_deg, dist_pc, method="ymw16")
272	    tau = tau_ne if method == "ne2001" else tau_yw
273	    try:
274	        tau_ms = float(tau.to(u.ms).value)
275	    except (AttributeError, u.UnitConversionError):
276	        tau_ms = float(tau) * 1.0e3
277	    return (float(dm_ne.value), float(dm_yw.value), tau_ms)
278	
279	
```

1	#!/usr/bin/env python
2	"""Radiometer flux calibration: per-channel S/N -> Jy for FLITS dynamic spectra.
3	
4	FLITS data is per-channel z-scored S/N (scattering/scat_analysis/pipeline/io.py:131-146:
5	_bandpass_correct divides each channel by its own off-pulse mean/std and the loader keeps
6	"units as S/N"). Physical flux density is therefore one radiometer multiply away:
7	
8	    S_nu(nu,t) [Jy] = (S/N)(nu,t) * sigma_S(nu),
9	    sigma_S(nu) = SEFD(nu) / (sqrt(n_pol * dnu * dt) * G(theta,phi,nu)),
10	
11	with SEFD = 2 k_B T_sys / A_eff and G the beam gain (boresight=1). Dividing by the
12	per-channel off-pulse noise also cancels the bandpass gain, so the S/N spectrum (and hence
13	this calibration) is free of the band-edge rolloff that rails the fitted gamma_D.
14	
15	The band fluence integral (Jy*ms*Hz) returned here feeds
16	analysis/calculate_burst_energies.band_energy_erg with flux_scale=1 -- the per-channel scale
17	is already folded in. See docs/rse/specs/plan-radiometer-flux-cal.md.
18	"""
19	
20	from __future__ import annotations
21	
22	import numpy as np
23	
24	
25	def radiometer_sigma_jy(sefd_jy, n_pol, dnu_hz, dt_s, g):
26	    """Per-sample radiometer noise [Jy]: SEFD / (sqrt(n_pol*dnu*dt) * G).
27	
28	    sefd_jy: system-equivalent flux density [Jy]; n_pol: summed polarisations;
29	    dnu_hz: channel bandwidth [Hz]; dt_s: sample time [s]; g: beam gain (boresight=1).
30	    """
31	    return sefd_jy / (np.sqrt(n_pol * dnu_hz * dt_s) * g)
32	
33	
34	def calibrated_band_integral_jy_ms_hz(sn_integrated, sigma_jy, freq_hz, dt_ms):
35	    """int_band [ sigma_S(nu) * dt_ms * sum_onpulse(S/N)(nu) ] dnu   [Jy*ms*Hz].
36	
37	    sn_integrated: per-channel sum over the on-pulse window of (data/noise_std) [dimensionless];
38	    sigma_jy: per-channel sigma_S [Jy]; freq_hz: ascending channel centres [Hz]; dt_ms: sample [ms].
39	    """
40	    chan_fluence_jy_ms = sigma_jy * dt_ms * sn_integrated  # [Jy*ms] per channel
41	    return float(np.trapezoid(chan_fluence_jy_ms, freq_hz))  # [Jy*ms*Hz]
42	
43	
44	def sn_spectrum_from_npy(inpath, telescope, f_factor=1, t_factor=1, onpulse_thresh=3.0):
45	    """Per-channel on-pulse S/N spectrum from a burst .npy via the FLITS loader.
46	
47	    Returns (freq_hz ascending, sn_integrated [per channel, sum over on-pulse of data/noise_std],
48	    dt_ms, dnu_hz). BurstDataset z-scores each channel (io.py:131-146), so model.data is already
49	    S/N; dividing by the full-window per-channel noise makes the unit explicit and robust to the
50	    downsample rescaling.
51	    """
52	    import sys
53	    from pathlib import Path
54	
55	    repo = Path(__file__).resolve().parents[1]
56	    sys.path.insert(0, str(repo / "scattering"))  # io.py uses package-relative imports (..burstfit)
57	    from scat_analysis.config_utils import load_telescope_block
58	    from scat_analysis.pipeline.io import BurstDataset
59	
60	    tel = load_telescope_block(str(repo / "scattering" / "configs" / "telescopes.yaml"), telescope)
61	    ds = BurstDataset(
62	        inpath,
63	        inpath,
64	        telescope=tel,
65	        f_factor=f_factor,
66	        t_factor=t_factor,
67	        onpulse_crop=True,
68	        onpulse_thresh=onpulse_thresh,
69	    )
70	    m = ds.model
71	    noise = np.clip(m.noise_std, 1e-9, None)  # per-channel full-window noise (n_freq,)
72	    sn = m.data / noise[:, None]  # (n_freq, n_time_onpulse) signal-to-noise
73	    sn_integrated = np.nansum(sn, axis=1)  # sum over the cropped on-pulse window
74	    return m.freq * 1e9, sn_integrated, ds.dt_ms, ds.df_MHz * 1e6
75	
76	
77	def dsa_sigma_jy(freq_hz, dnu_hz, sefd_jy, dt_s, theta_deg, phi_deg, beam_gain_fn):
78	    """Per-channel radiometer noise sigma_S(nu) [Jy] for DSA (n_pol=2) at a beam offset.
79	
80	    beam_gain_fn(theta_deg, phi_deg, freq_ghz) -> normalized power-beam gain (boresight=1);
81	    dnu_hz is the channel bandwidth (downsampled ds.df_MHz*1e6), dt_s the sample time [s].
82	    """
83	    g = np.array([beam_gain_fn(theta_deg, phi_deg, f / 1e9) for f in freq_hz])
84	    return radiometer_sigma_jy(sefd_jy, 2, dnu_hz, dt_s, g)
85	
86	
87	def dsa_beam_offset(dec_src, dec_pointing):
88	    """(theta_deg, phi_deg) of the source from the DSA primary-beam boresight.
89	
90	    DSA-110 is a transit array: at the burst's meridian transit (HA~0) the boresight sits at the
91	    source RA, so the angular separation is exactly the declination difference; phi=0 (meridian).
92	    HA refinement (small E-W term) is out of scope -- see the plan.
93	    """
94	    return abs(dec_src - dec_pointing), 0.0
95	
96	
97	def burst_epoch_position(nick):
98	    """(mjd, ra_deg, dec_deg) for a burst nickname from configs/bursts.yaml."""
99	    from pathlib import Path
100	
101	    import yaml
102	
103	    b = yaml.safe_load(
104	        (Path(__file__).resolve().parents[1] / "configs" / "bursts.yaml").read_text()
105	    )
106	    e = b["bursts"][nick]
107	    return float(e["mjd"]), float(e["ra_deg"]), float(e["dec_deg"])
108	
109	
110	def _csv_lookup(filename, key, key_col="burst", val_col=None):
111	    import csv
112	    from pathlib import Path
113	
114	    p = Path(__file__).resolve().parents[1] / "analysis" / "burst_energies" / filename
115	    if not p.exists():
116	        raise FileNotFoundError(f"{p} missing -- run the Phase 4 acquisition")
117	    for row in csv.DictReader(p.open()):
118	        if row[key_col] == key:
119	            return row[val_col]
120	    raise KeyError(f"{key} not in {p}")
121	
122	
123	def load_dsa_sefd(nick):
124	    """Measured DSA SEFD [Jy] nearest the burst epoch, from dsa_sefd.csv (Phase 4)."""
125	    return float(_csv_lookup("dsa_sefd.csv", nick, val_col="sefd_jy"))
126	
127	
128	def dsa_pointing_dec(nick):
129	    """DSA primary-beam pointing declination [deg] for a burst, from dsa_pointing.csv (Phase 4)."""
130	    return float(_csv_lookup("dsa_pointing.csv", nick, val_col="pointing_dec_deg"))
131	
132	
133	# DSA-110 formed its FRB detection beam by coherently combining 48 antennas in 2022
134	# (Law et al. 2024, arXiv:2307.03344). The dsa110-rt SEFD store is PER-BASELINE (~per-element;
135	# estimate_sefd.py:compute_sefd_per_baseline), so the coherent-beam SEFD is SEFD_element / N_ant.
136	# Cross-check: this beam SEFD (~167 Jy) reproduces the catalog fluences (16.2/26.2/13.2 Jy*ms for
137	# zach/whitney/oran) to within the ~2x boxcar-vs-full-integral estimator difference; the empirical
138	# matched-filter anchor is ~234 Jy (=> effective N_eff~34, ~70% beamforming efficiency).
139	DSA_N_ANT = 48
140	
141	
142	def load_dsa_sefd_beam(nick):
143	    """Coherent-beam SEFD [Jy] = per-element dashboard SEFD / N_ant (the detection beam)."""
144	    return load_dsa_sefd(nick) / DSA_N_ANT
145	
146	
147	def _dsa_burst_config(nick):
148	    """(npy_path, f_factor, t_factor) from configs/batch/dsa/<nick>_dsa.yaml (the fit's own binning)."""
149	    from pathlib import Path
150	
151	    import yaml
152	
153	    cfg = Path(__file__).resolve().parents[1] / "configs" / "batch" / "dsa" / f"{nick}_dsa.yaml"
154	    if not cfg.exists():
155	        raise FileNotFoundError(f"{cfg} missing -- no DSA batch config for {nick}")
156	    c = yaml.safe_load(cfg.read_text())
157	    return (cfg.parent / c["path"]).resolve(), int(c.get("f_factor", 1)), int(c.get("t_factor", 1))
158	
159	
160	def dsa_band_fluence_jy_ms_hz(nick):
161	    """Calibrated DSA-band fluence integral [Jy*ms*Hz] for a burst (Phases 2-3 composed).
162	
163	    Loads the burst .npy via its batch config (same f/t binning the joint fit used), turns the
164	    per-channel on-pulse S/N spectrum into Jy with sigma_S(nu)=SEFD_beam/(sqrt(2*dnu*dt)*G(theta,nu)),
165	    and integrates over the DSA band. SEFD_beam is the COHERENT-beam SEFD (per-element / N_ant);
166	    using the per-element SEFD directly over-estimates the fluence by ~N_ant. Raises
167	    FileNotFoundError if the .npy is not staged locally (data/dsa/ is external -- iacobus:burst_npys).
168	    """
169	    from analysis.dsa_beam import beam_gain
170	
171	    npy, f_factor, t_factor = _dsa_burst_config(nick)
172	    if not npy.exists():
173	        raise FileNotFoundError(
174	            f"{npy} not materialized -- stage the DSA .npy (iacobus:burst_npys) under data/dsa/"
175	        )
176	    freq_hz, sn_int, dt_ms, dnu_hz = sn_spectrum_from_npy(npy, "dsa", f_factor, t_factor)
177	    _mjd, _ra, dec_src = burst_epoch_position(nick)
178	    theta, phi = dsa_beam_offset(dec_src, dsa_pointing_dec(nick))
179	    sigma_jy = dsa_sigma_jy(
180	        freq_hz, dnu_hz, load_dsa_sefd_beam(nick), dt_ms / 1e3, theta, phi, beam_gain
181	    )
182	    return calibrated_band_integral_jy_ms_hz(sn_int, sigma_jy, freq_hz, dt_ms)
183	
184	
185	def _check() -> None:
186	    # 1. radiometer noise vs analytic: 2000/sqrt(2*1e6*1e-3) == sqrt(2000); G=0.5 doubles it
187	    s = radiometer_sigma_jy(2000.0, 2, 1e6, 1e-3, 1.0)
188	    assert abs(s - np.sqrt(2000.0)) < 1e-9, s
189	    assert abs(radiometer_sigma_jy(2000.0, 2, 1e6, 1e-3, 0.5) - 2.0 * s) < 1e-9
190	    # 2. flat-band integral oracle: trapz(const) == const*(nu2-nu1)
191	    freq_hz = np.linspace(1.311e9, 1.499e9, 64)
192	    i_band = calibrated_band_integral_jy_ms_hz(
193	        np.full(64, 3.0), np.full(64, 5.0), freq_hz, 0.131072
194	    )
195	    oracle = 3.0 * 5.0 * 0.131072 * (freq_hz[-1] - freq_hz[0])
196	    assert abs(i_band - oracle) / oracle < 1e-9, (i_band, oracle)
197	    print("self-check OK: radiometer noise + flat-band integral match analytic oracles")
198	
199	
200	if __name__ == "__main__":
201	    _check()
202

64	def chime_sigma_jy(freq_hz, dnu_hz, sefd_jy, dt_s, g=1.0):
65	    """Per-channel radiometer noise sigma_S(nu) [Jy] for CHIME (n_pol=2).
66	
67	    g is the beam gain at the source (default 1.0: baseband burst formed at its position). Pass a
68	    scalar or per-channel array from beam_gain(...) for an off-centre source. sefd_jy may be a
69	    scalar (frequency-flat documented SEFD) or per-channel.
70	    """
71	    return radiometer_sigma_jy(sefd_jy, 2, dnu_hz, dt_s, g)
72	
73	
74	# Documented zenith SEFD from Amiri+2018 Table 1: SEFD = 2 k_B Tsys / A_eff.
75	#   2 k_B = 2761.3 Jy m^2 / K; Tsys = 50 K (receiver noise, Table 1); A_eff = eta * 8000 m^2
76	#   (physical collecting area, Table 1) with aperture efficiency eta = 0.5 (typical cylinder).
77	#   -> SEFD_zenith ~ 2761.3 * 50 / (0.5 * 8000) = 34.5 Jy.
78	# Systematic: eta in [0.4,0.6] and real Tsys (sky+ground) up to ~80 K give SEFD ~29-69 Jy (~0.25
79	# dex); the declination (zenith-angle, |dec-49.32|<~25 deg for these bursts) and band-edge Tsys/A_eff
80	# dependence are also inside this band -- hence the frequency-flat constant.
81	TWO_KB_JY_M2_PER_K = 2.0 * 1.380649e-23 / 1e-26  # = 2761.298 Jy m^2 / K
82	
83	
84	def sefd_zenith_jy(tsys_k=50.0, a_phys_m2=8000.0, eta=0.5):
85	    """CHIME zenith SEFD [Jy] = 2 k_B Tsys / (eta * A_phys). See the note above for provenance."""
86	    return TWO_KB_JY_M2_PER_K * tsys_k / (eta * a_phys_m2)
87	
88	
89	def load_chime_sefd(nick=None):
90	    """Documented CHIME SEFD [Jy] from analysis/burst_energies/chime_sefd.csv (single row).
91	
92	    nick is accepted for call-site symmetry with load_dsa_sefd but ignored: CHIME re-points onto
93	    each burst, so a single zenith value (with the systematic above) is used for all.
94	    """

def main():
    b = sys.argv[1]
    C1 = float(sys.argv[2]) if len(sys.argv) > 2 else 1.0
    npz = np.load(f"{J}/{b}_joint_samples.npz", allow_pickle=True)
    if "gain_C" not in npz:
        sys.exit(f"{b}: no saved gains (not a gain-marginalized fit)")
    d = json.load(open(f"{J}/{b}_joint_fit.json"))
    tau1 = d["percentiles"]["tau_1ghz"]["median"]
    al = d["percentiles"]["alpha"]["median"]
    print(f"{b}: tau_1GHz={tau1:.3f}ms alpha={al:.2f}  (C1={C1})")
    res = {}
    fig, ax = plt.subplots(1, 2, figsize=(11, 4))
    for k, (fkey, gkey, name, nu_band) in enumerate(
            [("freq_C", "gain_C", "CHIME", 0.6), ("freq_D", "gain_D", "DSA", 1.405)]):
        r = analyse(npz[fkey], npz[gkey], name)
        res[name] = r
        if r:
            ax[k].plot(r["lags"], r["ac"], "k.-", ms=4, label="ACF")
            if r["dnud"] == r["dnud"]:
                xx = np.linspace(0, r["lags"].max(), 200)
                ax[k].plot(xx, lorentz(xx, r["dnud"]), "r", label=f"Lorentz dnud={r['dnud']:.1f}MHz")
            ax[k].axhline(0.5, color="0.7", ls=":"); ax[k].set_xlabel("freq lag (MHz)")
            ax[k].set_title(f"{b} {name} gain ACF"); ax[k].legend(fontsize=8)
    # same-screen test per band
    print("  SAME-SCREEN test (tau and scintillation from one thin screen => equal):")
    for name, nu in [("CHIME", 0.6), ("DSA", 1.405)]:
        r = res.get(name)
        if not r or r["dnud"] != r["dnud"]:
            continue
        tau_nu = tau1 * nu ** (-al)                          # ms
        dnud_pred = C1 / (2 * np.pi * tau_nu) * 1e3 / 1e3    # MHz: tau ms -> 1/(2pi tau ms) = kHz? fix below
        # tau in ms -> 1/(2 pi tau[s]) Hz; tau_ms*1e-3 s; dnud = C1/(2 pi tau_s) Hz -> /1e6 MHz
        dnud_pred = C1 / (2 * np.pi * (tau_nu * 1e-3)) / 1e6
        print(f"    {name} @ {nu:.2f}GHz: tau={tau_nu*1e3:.1f}us  "
```

1	"""
2	burstfit_joint.py
3	=================
4	
5	Joint two-telescope scattering fit (CHIME ~0.6 GHz + DSA ~1.4 GHz).
6	
7	Single-band M3 fits with alpha fixed = 4 give *inconsistent* tau_1ghz between
8	CHIME and DSA for the same sightline (observed up to ~15x), which is only
9	possible if the scattering index alpha != 4. A single band cannot separate
10	alpha from tau (the tau-alpha degeneracy): tau(nu) = tau_1ghz * nu^-alpha, and
11	one band fixes the product at its own frequency, not the slope.
12	
13	This module fits both bands simultaneously with a *shared* (tau_1ghz, alpha) and
14	*per-telescope* (c0, t0, gamma, zeta, delta_dm). The ~1 GHz lever arm between
15	the two bands measures alpha directly: the ratio tau_C/tau_D pins the slope,
16	the shared tau_1ghz pins the normalization at 1 GHz.
17	
18	12-parameter vector (M3 both bands, alpha free):
19	
20	    [tau_1ghz, alpha | c0_C, t0_C, gamma_C, zeta_C, ddm_C | c0_D, t0_D, gamma_D, zeta_D, ddm_D]
21	     ^shared sightline   ^CHIME intrinsic/timing            ^DSA intrinsic/timing
22	
23	Independent noise -> the joint log-likelihood is the sum of the two single-band
24	Gaussian log-likelihoods (reuses the nch^2-fixed FRBModel.log_likelihood).
25	
26	zeta is kept per-telescope (not shared) in the default paths: intrinsic width is
27	achromatic in principle, but the measured zeta also absorbs unmodelled per-band
28	structure, so the conservative choice is to let each band fit its own. The alpha
29	lever arm comes from the shared tau, independent of how zeta is treated.
30	
31	The ``shared_zeta`` path replaces the two per-band widths with ONE source width
32	law zeta(nu) = zeta_1ghz * nu^x_zeta evaluated per channel across the full band
33	(physically: the emission width belongs to the source, not the telescope, and
34	pulsars show it evolving with frequency -- radius-to-frequency mapping). This
35	makes the joint model a single coherent burst from the DSA top to the CHIME
36	bottom (used by the unified full-band figure) at the cost of an x_zeta-alpha
37	degeneracy: validate the x_zeta-alpha posterior covariance before trusting alpha.
38	
39	Usage
40	-----
41	```python
42	from burstfit_joint import fit_joint_scattering
43	res = fit_joint_scattering(
44	    model_C=model_chime, init_C=init_chime,
45	    model_D=model_dsa,   init_D=init_dsa,
```

802	def _weighted_percentiles(
803	    samples: NDArray[np.floating],
804	    weights: NDArray[np.floating],
805	    names: tuple[str, ...] = JOINT_PARAM_NAMES,
806	) -> dict[str, dict[str, float]]:
807	    """Weighted 16/50/84 percentiles per column (mirror NestedSamplingResult)."""
808	    out: dict[str, dict[str, float]] = {}
809	    for i, name in enumerate(names):
810	        s = samples[:, i]
811	        idx = np.argsort(s)
812	        ss, sw = s[idx], weights[idx]
813	        cdf = np.cumsum(sw)
814	        cdf /= cdf[-1]
815	        p16, p50, p84 = ss[np.searchsorted(cdf, [0.16, 0.50, 0.84])]
816	        out[name] = {
817	            "median": float(p50),
818	            "lower": float(p16),
819	            "upper": float(p84),
820	            "err_minus": float(p50 - p16),
821	            "err_plus": float(p84 - p50),
822	        }
823	    return out

```json
{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/Users/jakobfaber/Developer/repos/github.com/jakobtfaber/dsa110-FLITS/scattering/scat_analysis/burstfit_joint.py",
  "old_string": "def fit_joint_scattering(\n    *,",
  "new_string": "# --- Quality gate: the trust boundary for energy / DM-budget consumers --------\n# Mirrors the single-band FRBModel Level-1 physics gate (1.5 < alpha < 6.0;\n# 1e-4 < tau < 100 ms; .cursor/rules/AGENT_CONFIGURATION_FLITS.md). A joint alpha\n# whose posterior median pins to a prior wall is prior-shaped, not measured\n# (analysis/scattering-refit-2026-06/JOINT_FIT_STATE.md), so it FAILs no matter\n# how tight the formal error bar looks -- the lever arm collapsed.\nALPHA_PHYS_MIN, ALPHA_PHYS_MAX = 1.5, 6.0\nTAU_PHYS_MIN_MS, TAU_PHYS_MAX_MS = 1e-4, 100.0\n\n\ndef joint_quality_flag(\n    summary: dict[str, Any],\n    *,\n    rail_frac: float = 0.05,\n    rel_err_max: float = 0.5,\n) -> str:\n    \"\"\"Return PASS / MARGINAL / FAIL for a joint-fit summary dict.\n\n    ``summary`` carries ``alpha={median,err_minus,err_plus}``,\n    ``tau_1ghz={median,...}`` and ``alpha_bounds=[lo,hi]`` (as written by\n    run_joint_fit). The gate is the single source of truth used by run_joint_fit,\n    the committed-JSON re-gate, and the energy/budget readers so a railed fit\n    cannot silently flow into E_iso.\n\n    - FAIL: alpha or tau outside physical bounds, or alpha pinned within\n      ``rail_frac`` of either prior edge (prior-shaped, not measured).\n    - MARGINAL: alpha interior but weakly constrained (relative error >\n      ``rel_err_max``) or its 1.5-sigma interval still reaches a prior edge ->\n      report as a limit, not a measurement.\n    - PASS: alpha interior, precise, and 1.5-sigma clear of both prior edges.\n    \"\"\"\n    try:\n        a = summary[\"alpha\"]\n        a_med = float(a[\"median\"])\n        a_lo = abs(float(a[\"err_minus\"]))\n        a_hi = abs(float(a[\"err_plus\"]))\n        tau_med = float(summary[\"tau_1ghz\"][\"median\"])\n        lo, hi = (float(x) for x in summary[\"alpha_bounds\"])\n    except (KeyError, TypeError, ValueError):\n        return \"FAIL\"\n    if not (np.isfinite(a_med) and np.isfinite(tau_med)):\n        return \"FAIL\"\n    if not (TAU_PHYS_MIN_MS < tau_med < TAU_PHYS_MAX_MS):\n        return \"FAIL\"\n    if not (ALPHA_PHYS_MIN < a_med < ALPHA_PHYS_MAX):\n        return \"FAIL\"\n    span = hi - lo\n    if span > 0 and (a_med - lo <= rail_frac * span or hi - a_med <= rail_frac * span):\n        return \"FAIL\"\n    rel_err = max(a_lo, a_hi) / a_med if a_med > 0 else float(\"inf\")\n    touches_edge = (a_med - 1.5 * a_lo) <= lo or (a_med + 1.5 * a_hi) >= hi\n    if rel_err > rel_err_max or touches_edge:\n        return \"MARGINAL\"\n    return \"PASS\"\n\n\ndef fit_joint_scattering(\n    *,"
}

{

  "file_path": "/Users/jakobfaber/Developer/repos/github.com/jakobtfaber/dsa110-FLITS/analysis/calculate_burst_energies.py",
  "old_string": "def load_joint_params() -> dict[str, dict]:\n    \"\"\"nickname (lowercased) -> {c0_C, gamma_C, c0_D, gamma_D} medians.\"\"\"\n    out = {}\n    for p in sorted(JOINT_DIR.glob(\"*_joint_fit.json\")):\n        d = json.loads(p.read_text())\n        pct = d[\"percentiles\"]\n        try:\n            out[d[\"burst\"].lower()] = {\n                k: pct[k][\"median\"] for k in (\"c0_C\", \"gamma_C\", \"c0_D\", \"gamma_D\")\n            }\n        except KeyError:\n            continue  # not a per-band-amplitude fit; skip\n    return out",
  "new_string": "def load_joint_params() -> dict[str, dict]:\n    \"\"\"nickname (lowercased) -> {c0_C, gamma_C, c0_D, gamma_D, quality_flag} medians.\n\n    Trust boundary: a joint fit whose shared-alpha posterior rails the prior is\n    prior-shaped, not measured (joint_quality_flag == FAIL), and its per-band\n    c0/gamma were fit jointly UNDER that wrong alpha, so they are refused here --\n    a railed fit must not flow into E_iso. FAIL fits are dropped (logged, not\n    silently); MARGINAL fits are kept but flagged so the table can mark them.\n    \"\"\"\n    out = {}\n    dropped: list[str] = []\n    for p in sorted(JOINT_DIR.glob(\"*_joint_fit.json\")):\n        d = json.loads(p.read_text())\n        flag = d.get(\"quality_flag\")\n        if flag is None:  # un-stamped legacy JSON: derive the gate, don't assume PASS\n            from scat_analysis.burstfit_joint import joint_quality_flag\n\n            flag = joint_quality_flag(d)\n        if flag == \"FAIL\":\n            dropped.append(f\"{d.get('burst')} (alpha={d['alpha']['median']:.2f})\")\n            continue\n        pct = d[\"percentiles\"]\n        try:\n            row = {k: pct[k][\"median\"] for k in (\"c0_C\", \"gamma_C\", \"c0_D\", \"gamma_D\")}\n        except KeyError:\n            continue  # not a per-band-amplitude fit; skip\n        row[\"quality_flag\"] = flag\n        out[d[\"burst\"].lower()] = row\n    if dropped:\n        print(\n            f\"[gate] refused {len(dropped)} joint fit(s) with FAIL quality \"\n            f\"(railed/unphysical alpha): {', '.join(dropped)}\",\n            file=sys.stderr,\n        )\n    return out"
}

```
300	\subsection{{Isotropic-equivalent energies}}
301	\label{{sec:burst-energies}}
302	
303	For each sightline with a spectroscopic host redshift we estimate the
304	isotropic-equivalent burst energy directly from the joint CHIME--DSA fit,
305	without extrapolating either band's spectrum beyond where it is constrained.
306	The joint fit returns a per-band spectral amplitude and index,
307	$F_X(\nu) = c_{{0,X}}\,(\nu/\nu_{{\mathrm{{ref}},X}})^{{\gamma_X}}$, which we put on
308	an absolute scale with each telescope's flux calibration $s_X$
309	($\mathrm{{Jy}}$ per native unit) and integrate over its own observing
310	band---CHIME over $0.400$--$0.800\,\mathrm{{GHz}}$ and DSA over
311	$1.311$--$1.499\,\mathrm{{GHz}}$,
312	%
313	\begin{{equation}}
314	E_{{\mathrm{{iso}}}} = \frac{{4\pi D_L^2(z)}}{{1+z}}\left[
315	  s_C\!\int_{{\nu_1^{{C}}}}^{{\nu_2^{{C}}}} F_{{\mathrm{{CHIME}}}}(\nu)\,d\nu
316	  + s_D\!\int_{{\nu_1^{{D}}}}^{{\nu_2^{{D}}}} F_{{\mathrm{{DSA}}}}(\nu)\,d\nu
317	\right],
318	\label{{eq:eiso}}
319	\end{{equation}}
320	%
321	with $D_L(z)$ from the fiducial \textit{{Planck}}\,2018 cosmology, the $(1+z)$
322	bandwidth k-correction applied, and
323	$1\,\mathrm{{Jy\,ms\,Hz}} = 10^{{-29}}\,\mathrm{{J\,m^{{-2}}}}$. Bursts whose hosts
324	lack a spectroscopic redshift are omitted. This band-restricted integral
325	constrains the energy released \emph{{within the observed spectral envelope}}: it
326	avoids the large, model-dependent extrapolation that a single power law fit
327	across the full $0.4$--$1.5\,\mathrm{{GHz}}$ span would require, at the cost of not
328	counting flux in the unobserved $0.8$--$1.3\,\mathrm{{GHz}}$ gap (so the values are
329	lower limits on the $0.4$--$1.5\,\mathrm{{GHz}}$ energy).
330	
331	\begin{{table}}
332	\centering
333	\caption{{Isotropic-equivalent energies of the co-detected FRBs, integrated over
334	the CHIME ($0.4$--$0.8\,\mathrm{{GHz}}$) and DSA ($1.31$--$1.50\,\mathrm{{GHz}}$) bands
335	from the joint scattering fit and put on an absolute scale with each band's flux
336	calibration. $\gamma_C,\gamma_D$ are the per-band spectral indices;
337	$E_{{\mathrm{{iso}}}}$ includes the $(1+z)$ k-correction.}}
338	\label{{tab:burst-energies}}
339	\begin{{tabular}}{{lccccc}}
```

Bursts whose hosts\nlack a spectroscopic redshift are omitted. This band-restricted integral\nconstrains the energy released \\emph{{within the observed spectral envelope}}: it\navoids the large, model-dependent extrapolation that a single power law fit\nacross the full $0.4$--$1.5\\,\\mathrm{{GHz}}$ span would require, at the cost of not\ncounting flux in the unobserved $0.8$--$1.3\\,\\mathrm{{GHz}}$ gap (so the values are\nlower limits on the $0.4$--$1.5\\,\\mathrm{{GHz}}$ energy). The absolute flux scale\ncarries a $\\sim$\\,{E_ISO_SYS_DEX}\\,dex systematic, dominated by the DSA\ncoherent-beam SEFD (per-element value divided by the formed-beam antenna count)\nand the boxcar-versus-integral fluence estimator (with a further $\\sim$0.25\\,dex\nfor CHIME once its band is calibrated); the energies below are therefore accurate\nonly to a factor of a few in their absolute normalization. Each fit's\nshared-$\\alpha$ quality flag is listed (only PASS/MARGINAL joint fits are\ntabulated; railed-$\\alpha$ FAIL fits are excluded)."

```json
{
  "file_path": "/Users/jakobfaber/Developer/repos/github.com/jakobtfaber/dsa110-FLITS/analysis/scattering-refit-2026-06/regate_joint_json.py",
  "content": "#!/usr/bin/env python\n\"\"\"Stamp quality_flag (PASS/MARGINAL/FAIL) onto the committed joint_json/*.json.\n\nThe joint fits in joint_json/ were written before run_joint_fit stamped a quality\nflag, and several rail the shared-alpha prior (alpha pinned to 1.0/6.0) -- those\nare prior-shaped, not measured (JOINT_FIT_STATE.md). This applies the single\nsource-of-truth gate (burstfit_joint.joint_quality_flag) so downstream readers\n(calculate_burst_energies, sightline budget) refuse railed fits. Idempotent.\n\n    python regate_joint_json.py [--dry-run]\n\"\"\"\n\nimport json\nimport os\nimport sys\n\nREPO = os.environ.get(\"FLITS_REPO\") or os.path.abspath(\n    os.path.join(os.path.dirname(__file__), \"..\", \"..\")\n)\nsys.path.insert(0, os.path.join(REPO, \"scattering\"))\nfrom scat_analysis.burstfit_joint import joint_quality_flag  # noqa: E402\n\nJDIR = os.path.join(os.path.dirname(__file__), \"joint_json\")\n\n\ndef main() -> None:\n    dry = \"--dry-run\" in sys.argv\n    for fn in sorted(os.listdir(JDIR)):\n        if not fn.endswith(\"_joint_fit.json\"):\n            continue\n        path = os.path.join(JDIR, fn)\n        d = json.load(open(path))\n        flag = joint_quality_flag(d)\n        old = d.get(\"quality_flag\")\n        a = d[\"alpha\"][\"median\"]\n        print(f\"{d['burst']:11s} alpha={a:6.3f}  {old or '(none)':8s} -> {flag}\")\n        if not dry and old != flag:\n            d[\"quality_flag\"] = flag\n            json.dump(d, open(path, \"w\"), indent=2)\n\n\nif __name__ == \"__main__\":\n    main()\n"
}

We adopt the lower-middle 0.11 (the 0.13 high end\n# inflates the DM); even so the isothermal beta-model over-predicts the outskirts,\n# so dm_cluster_beta_model returns an UPPER BOUND (see its docstring).\nCLUSTER_F_GAS_500 = 0.11\n# Fully ionized ICM mean molecular weight per electron (primordial-ish).\nCLUSTER_MU_E = 1.17\n# R200/R500 = (M200/M500 * 500/200)^(1/3) = (1.3*2.5)^(1/3) = 1.48, consistent with\n# config.CLUSTER_M500_TO_M200 = 1.3 (typical c500 ~ 1.5). The earlier 1.54 assumed a\n# different concentration and was inconsistent with the 1.3 mass ratio used for R200.\nCLUSTER_R200_OVER_R500 = 1.48"
}

  "new_string": "    Isothermal beta-model n_e(r) = n_e0 [1+(r/r_c)^2]^(-3beta/2) (Cavaliere &\n    Fusco-Femiano 1976), normalized so the hot gas within R500 is f_gas * M500\n    (Vikhlinin+2006). The column is projected at impact parameter b, truncated at\n    r_trunc_factor * R500, and divided by (1+z) for the observer frame\n    (Macquart+2020 convention). Returns 0.0 for b beyond truncation or bad inputs.\n\n    UPPER BOUND: the isothermal beta-model keeps the density slope fixed to large\n    radius, but real clusters steepen beyond ~R500, so this over-predicts the\n    projected column by ~1.5-2x relative to the resolved anchor -- Lee+2023 give\n    ~300 and ~110 pc cm^-3 at b ~ 0.4 and 0.8 R200 for the FRB 20190520B foreground\n    clusters, where this model returns ~1.7-1.9x more. Treat the value as a\n    conservative ceiling on the cluster DM, not a point estimate; the upgrade path\n    is a Vikhlinin+2006 steepening term or a per-cluster X-ray n_e(r) profile.\n    \"\"\""
}

{

  "old_string": "# Cap on the photo-z 1-sigma used in the foreground buffer. DESI floor/leak rows\n# carry absurd e_zphot (sigma_z = 0.4-1.7) that let any background galaxy pass the\n# 2-sigma cut; capping at a realistic Legacy scatter stops z>z_FRB leakage while\n# keeping genuine boundary photo-z galaxies.\nMAX_PHOTOZ_Z_ERR = 0.05",
  "new_string": "# Cap on the photo-z 1-sigma used in the foreground buffer. The DESI Legacy Surveys\n# photo-z scatter is sigma_z/(1+z) ~ 0.02-0.05 over the depth and colour range of\n# these fields (Zou+2019 ApJS 242,8; Duncan 2022 MNRAS 512,3662), so 0.05 is the\n# *physical* faint-end 1-sigma -- not a knob tuned to a result. DESI floor/leak rows\n# instead carry e_zphot = 0.4-1.7 (catastrophic/placeholder values) that at 2-sigma\n# would rescue any background galaxy as \"foreground\"; capping at the real Legacy\n# scatter rejects that leakage while keeping genuine boundary photo-z galaxies. The\n# foreground sample's sensitivity to this cap and FOREGROUND_PHOTOZ_FLOOR should be\n# reported as a small grid in the sightline paper.\nMAX_PHOTOZ_Z_ERR = 0.05"
}

1	"""Regression tests for `_JointPriorTransformOrdered` per-band `dt_min`.
2	
3	The ordered transform sorts each band's t0 group and enforces a minimum component
4	separation. `dt_min` may be a scalar (broadcast to every group) or one float per
5	group (per-band floors). These tests assert the realized prior draws honor each
6	group's own floor, that the scalar path is unchanged, the degenerate-width branch
7	still collapses, and a length-mismatch sequence fails fast.
8	"""
9	
10	import numpy as np
11	import pytest
12	
13	from scattering.scat_analysis.burstfit_joint import _JointPriorTransformOrdered
14	
15	
16	def _spec(ndim, lo=0.0, hi=10.0):
17	    # spec entry = (name, (lo, hi), is_log_flag); only bounds + flag are read.
18	    return [(f"t0_{i}", (lo, hi), False) for i in range(ndim)]
19	
20	
21	def _min_gap(x, grp):
22	    g = np.sort(x[grp])
23	    return float(np.min(np.diff(g))) if g.size > 1 else np.inf
24	
25	
26	def test_per_group_floor_honored():
27	    """Each group's realized gaps respect ITS OWN dt_min, not the other group's."""
28	    groups = [np.array([0, 1]), np.array([2, 3, 4])]  # n=2 (C), n=3 (D)
29	    dtC, dtD = 2.0, 1.0
30	    tf = _JointPriorTransformOrdered(_spec(5), groups, dt_min=[dtC, dtD])
```

{
  "file_path": "/Users/jakobfaber/Developer/repos/github.com/jakobtfaber/dsa110-FLITS/tests/test_joint_quality_flag.py",
  "content": "\"\"\"Correctness tests for `joint_quality_flag` (the joint-fit trust boundary).\n\nCriterion: the gate must mirror the single-band FRBModel Level-1 physics gate\n(1.5 < alpha < 6.0; 1e-4 < tau < 100 ms) AND reject prior-railed alpha, so a\nfit whose shared-alpha posterior pins a prior wall cannot pass as a measurement\nand flow into E_iso. Expected verdicts are pinned to the real committed\njoint_json/*.json medians (the referee-confirmed split: only zach/phineas/wilhelm\nare clean measurements). Each case fails if the gate logic regresses.\n\"\"\"\n\nimport pytest\n\nfrom scattering.scat_analysis.burstfit_joint import joint_quality_flag\n\n\ndef _summary(alpha, em, ep, tau=0.3, bounds=(1.0, 6.0)):\n    return {\n        \"alpha\": {\"median\": alpha, \"err_minus\": em, \"err_plus\": ep},\n        \"tau_1ghz\": {\"median\": tau},\n        \"alpha_bounds\": list(bounds),\n    }\n\n\n# (label, alpha, err_minus, err_plus, expected) -- real committed medians.\nCASES = [\n    (\"zach\", 3.663, 0.017, 0.017, \"PASS\"),\n    (\"phineas\", 3.578, 0.041, 0.043, \"PASS\"),\n    (\"wilhelm\", 2.706, 0.047, 0.049, \"PASS\"),\n    (\"isha\", 4.961, 2.598, 0.849, \"MARGINAL\"),  # interior but rel-err > 0.5\n    (\"mahi\", 5.530, 0.419, 0.322, \"MARGINAL\"),  # 1.5-sigma upper tail reaches 6.0\n    (\"chromatica\", 5.999, 0.002, 0.001, \"FAIL\"),  # railed at ceiling\n    (\"freya\", 5.995, 0.008, 0.004, \"FAIL\"),\n    (\"hamilton\", 5.990, 0.016, 0.008, \"FAIL\"),\n    (\"johndoeII\", 1.373, 0.049, 0.044, \"FAIL\"),  # alpha < 1.5 physical floor\n    (\"oran\", 1.439, 0.325, 0.661, \"FAIL\"),\n    (\"whitney\", 1.458, 0.056, 0.055, \"FAIL\"),\n]\n\n\n@pytest.mark.parametrize(\"label,a,em,ep,expected\", CASES)\ndef test_committed_joint_fits_gate_as_expected(label, a, em, ep, expected):\n    assert joint_quality_flag(_summary(a, em, ep)) == expected, label\n\n\ndef test_tau_out_of_physical_bounds_fails():\n    # alpha is a clean Kolmogorov value, but tau below 1e-4 ms / above 100 ms FAILs.\n    assert joint_quality_flag(_summary(4.0, 0.05, 0.05, tau=5e-5)) == \"FAIL\"\n    assert joint_quality_flag(_summary(4.0, 0.05, 0.05, tau=150.0)) == \"FAIL\"\n    assert joint_quality_flag(_summary(4.0, 0.05, 0.05, tau=0.3)) == \"PASS\"\n\n\ndef test_rail_against_a_floor_equal_to_the_physical_bound_fails():\n    # With the prior floor set to the physical 1.5, a fit pinned just above it is\n    # still prior-shaped -> FAIL (the gate, not a high prior floor, rejects rails).\n    assert joint_quality_flag(_summary(1.55, 0.02, 0.02, bounds=(1.5, 6.0))) == \"FAIL\"\n    # ... but a genuinely interior shallow slope at the same bounds PASSes.\n    assert joint_quality_flag(_summary(2.6, 0.05, 0.05, bounds=(1.5, 6.0))) == \"PASS\"\n\n\ndef test_missing_or_malformed_summary_fails_closed():\n    assert joint_quality_flag({}) == \"FAIL\"\n    assert joint_quality_flag({\"alpha\": {\"median\": 4.0}}) == \"FAIL\"\n"
}

def test_g_scatt_has_intervening_geometry_and_zero_boundaries():
    near_front = sp.g_scatt(0.1, 0.5)
    near_source = sp.g_scatt(0.45, 0.5)

    assert sp.g_scatt(0.5, 0.5) == 0.0
    assert sp.g_scatt(0.6, 0.5) == 0.0
    assert sp.g_scatt(0.0, 0.5) == 0.0
    assert sp.g_scatt(None, 0.5) == 0.0
    assert near_front > 0.0
    assert near_source > 0.0
    assert near_source < near_front


    r200_ref, _, _ = get_rvir_and_rs(m200, z)
    assert abs(sp.r_delta_kpc(m200, z, 200) - r200_ref) / r200_ref < 1e-6
    # Higher overdensity -> smaller radius for the same mass.
    assert sp.r_delta_kpc(m200, z, 500) < sp.r_delta_kpc(m200, z, 200)
    assert math.isnan(sp.r_delta_kpc(-1.0, z, 200))


def test_dm_cluster_beta_model_matches_analytic_projection_untruncated():
    # For a huge truncation the LOS quadrature must match the closed-form Abel
    # projection of the beta-model: Sigma(b) = ne0 rc sqrt(pi) Gamma(3b/2-1/2)/
    # Gamma(3b/2) [1+(b/rc)^2]^((1-3b)/2). This isolates the quadrature.
    m500, z = 5.0e14, 0.25
    r500 = sp.r_delta_kpc(m500, z, 500)
    beta, rc, b = 0.65, 0.15 * r500, 0.05 * r500
    dm = sp.dm_cluster_beta_model(
        m500,
        z,
        b,
        r500_kpc=r500,
        beta=beta,
        rc_over_r500=0.15,
        f_gas=0.13,
        r_trunc_factor=1000.0,
    )
    ne0 = sp._beta_ne0_cm3(m500, z, r500, beta, rc, f_gas=0.13)
    sigma_kpc_cm3 = (
        ne0
        * rc
        * math.sqrt(math.pi)
        * math.gamma(1.5 * beta - 0.5)
        / math.gamma(1.5 * beta)
        * (1.0 + (b / rc) ** 2) ** (0.5 - 1.5 * beta)
    )
    dm_analytic = sigma_kpc_cm3 * 1000.0 / (1.0 + z)
    assert abs(dm - dm_analytic) / dm_analytic < 0.01


def test_dm_cluster_beta_model_zero_beyond_truncation():
    m500, z = 5.0e14, 0.25
    r500 = sp.r_delta_kpc(m500, z, 500)
    assert sp.dm_cluster_beta_model(m500, z, 1.54 * r500 + 1.0) == 0.0
    assert sp.dm_cluster_beta_model(-1.0, z, 100.0) == 0.0


def test_dm_cluster_beta_model_monotonic_and_linear_in_fgas():
    m500, z = 5.0e14, 0.25
    r500 = sp.r_delta_kpc(m500, z, 500)
    near = sp.dm_cluster_beta_model(m500, z, 0.1 * r500)
    far = sp.dm_cluster_beta_model(m500, z, 0.6 * r500)
    assert near > far > 0.0
    d1 = sp.dm_cluster_beta_model(m500, z, 0.2 * r500, f_gas=0.10)
    d2 = sp.dm_cluster_beta_model(m500, z, 0.2 * r500, f_gas=0.20)
    assert abs(d2 / d1 - 2.0) < 1e-6  # DM linear in gas fraction
```
